C-77

How much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

C5564th percentile

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: a meaningful amount is cuttable (in the order of 200 to 300bp of sales over three to four years beyond the current programme), mainly in SG&A and manufacturing. But management's stated preference is growth over margin, and the current programme is modest relative to the cost base ($130 million, about 1.2% of sales) and is becoming more expensive to deliver (costs of $230 million).

  • Cost of goods (about 36% of sales, about $3.9 billion): footprint (moving equipment assembly into China, lens production to tariff-neutral sites, closing or consolidating plants), automation (DSM Flex), procurement, and scale in new contact lens lines. Plausibly 50 to 100bp over three years beyond the programme, partly offset by tariffs and chip and resin inflation.
  • SG&A (about 33% of sales, about $3.6 billion): the largest pool. A heavy commercial machine ("an army of specialised team members" per expert panels) and 4,100 associates; shared services (2,500 staff) can grow further; artificial intelligence in operations. Cuttable by 100 to 200bp if management chose to put margin first. The 2018 to 2024 fall in opex from 49% to 41% of revenue shows that it can be done.
  • R&D (about 9% of sales, about $1 billion): cuttable toward 7% to 8% (peers: Sonova about 6%, Smith & Nephew about 5%), but this is the moat and the risk the Risks report names ("false friend: margin expansion masking R&D under-investment"). It should not be modelled as a lever.
  • The programme: $130 million run-rate savings (raised from $100 million in November 2026; about $50 million in 2026, the rest mainly in 2027); costs of $230 million (raised from $150 million). The incremental $30 million of savings costs about $80 million, which signals diminishing returns on the current scope.
  • Peer precedents: Carl Zeiss Meditec's ProfitUp (over €200 million gross by 2028/29, 1,000 roles); Smith & Nephew ($325 to $375 million gross by 2027); Sonova (CHF 90 million run-rate by year four for CHF 50 million of investment).
P-03

After assessing the scope for cost elimination and efficiency in greater detail, how much is likely to reach the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, and when?

D5049th percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: about $80 million of gross savings lands in 2027 and about $20 million more in 2028, but less than half of it reaches the core margin, because management has earmarked it to offset memory chip and resin inflation and tariffs. Net to margin we expect about 30 to 40bp in 2027 and about 10 to 15bp in 2028, and nothing from a new programme in our base. The larger, plausible scope (200 to 300bp over three to four years, mostly in SG&A) is an option that requires a change in management's stated preference for growth over margin, and nothing in the files signals that change. The restructuring costs, by contrast, do reach the statutory accounts in full: about $230 million in total, excluded from core.

The programme (Q3 2026 report, 10-Nov-26): expanded to about $230 million of costs (from $150 million) for $130 million of run-rate savings (from $100 million). About $50 million of savings landed in 2026; "the majority of the incremental savings" land in 2027 (CFO, 11-Nov-26). Implementation is "scheduled for completion by the end of 2026", so 2027 is the year the run rate is reached. In 9M 2026, $120 million was added to severance provisions, $77 million was paid, and $43 million remains. The incremental $30 million of savings costs about $80 million, a payback of about 2.7 years against about 1.5 years for the first $100 million: diminishing returns on the current scope.

Phasing to the P&L (our base):

2026 2027 2028 2029 to 2030
Gross savings in year (incremental) about $50m about $80m about $0 to $20m none assumed
Offsets earmarked (chips, resins, tariffs, wage inflation above price) partly (refund covered tariffs) about $35 to $45m small -

Restructuring charges (excluded from core) about $150m about $60 to $80m small -
Cash cost of restructuring about $120m about $80m small -

Why less than half reaches the margin: the company itself says the expanded savings are "primarily designated to help offset severe inflationary pressures" (interim report, 10-Nov-26). Bernstein puts chips and resins at about 20bp (about $23 million) for 2027; tariffs are a gross $80 to $100 million a year with no refund in 2027, plus about $80 million of capitalised tariffs flowing through cost of goods with a lag. The savings are therefore defensive: they protect the 2027 margin from falling rather than lift it.

Which lines it comes from: mostly personnel (severance provisions), across global departments, shared services and the field. The cost base is about $3.9 billion of cost of goods, $3.6 billion of SG&A and $1.0 billion of R&D. The 2018 to 2024 fall in opex from 49% to 41% of revenue (shared services from 200 to 2,000-2,500 staff) shows that SG&A can be cut; R&D should not be modelled as a lever because it is the moat.

The larger scope, and why it is not in our base:

  • SG&A: 100 to 200bp over three to four years is plausible (artificial intelligence in operations, further shared services, field-force restructuring as private-equity ASCs centralise purchasing), but management says "sustainable incremental revenue growth is more valuable than marginal operating margin gains" and the sales force is the bundling engine that protects surgical share.
  • Footprint (tariffs): moving equipment assembly into China and lens production to tariff-neutral sites; the CFO (January 2026) said it takes significant time and money; relief is unlikely before 2028 and risks write-downs if trade policy shifts again. We give it nothing in 2027 and treat it as an offset to tariff escalation rather than margin upside.
  • Manufacturing scale: the Singapore ($503 million) and Grosswallstadt ($162 million) lines lower contact lens unit costs as they fill (24 to 36 months); this is in our gross margin assumption, not in "cost elimination".

Peer precedents for timing and delivery: Carl Zeiss Meditec's ProfitUp (€160 million net by FY2028/29, up to €150 million of one-offs) delivers only €15 million in year one: back-end loaded. Smith & Nephew reached $330 million of cumulative savings more than a year early, but cut its 2026 revenue guide to 3% and trading profit from $1.3 billion to $1.25 billion in November 2026, showing that savings do not protect margins when volume disappoints. Sonova targets CHF 90 million by year four. Cooper cut SG&A through headcount reductions in FY2026 to offset weak sales. The precedents say self-help works fastest under external pressure (an activist at Smith & Nephew, a profit warning at Zeiss). Alcon has neither, which is why we expect the programme to be delivered on time but spent.

The option the Consumer should track: a new programme announced with the FY2027 guide or at a Capital Markets Day (none is scheduled in the files). A credible $150 to $200 million programme aimed at margin rather than inflation would add 130 to 170bp by 2029 to 2030 and is the cleanest route to consensus's 2028 to 2029 margins.

5e

Reinforcing feedback loops

2 answers
C-4772P-0546
C-47To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?B+7287th percentilefull answer under 4a Business quality
P-05

After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, such that dynamics at play today create additional scope for further positive/negative dynamics in the near/mid-term (reinforcing feedback loops), or the opposite (balancing loops), how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

D4633rd percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

The Investment Analyst's heading After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement: seven loops are at work. In 2027 the negative ones dominate revenue (the premium IOL trialing loop and the post-wave equipment trough), while the most powerful loop for the margin is a balancing one that management itself runs: it reinvests leverage as it appears. From 2028 the slow positive loops (the Unity installed base feeding consumables, contact lens capacity feeding share and unit cost, the buyback) carry the case. Net, the loops produce a 2027 trough in revenue growth (about 5%, H1 about 4%) and a gentle recovery to about 5.3% in 2028, without the re-acceleration that a resolved IOL loop would give. The decisive loop is the IOL one, and the evidence says it is a product-cycle loop that historically ended within 12 to 24 months of a competitive launch; whether Vivity Pro is that launch is unknowable before H1 2027 data.

Loop 1. Premium IOL surgeon trialing (reinforcing, negative, currently running). How it works: a rival launches a lens with a better profile (J&J's PureSee: non-diffractive, low dysphotopsia); surgeons trial it with free samples; good outcomes turn trials into habit; competitors deepen sampling where the incumbent looks weak; the incumbent's share falls further. Evidence now: United States PC-IOL share about 66% or lower (see Orientation, point 6), J&J's United States surgical vision +14% in Q3 2026 against Alcon implantables -2% cc; one California practice moved 65% to 70% of premium cases to PureSee (25-Sep-26); management concedes competitor sampling will remain intense for two more quarters. What stopped it in the past: a better Alcon optic. Share went from 55% (2009) to 35% (2019) when J&J's Symfony and others won, back above 80% (2021) on PanOptix, down to 66% (Q1 2025) on Odyssey and Envy, up to 70% to 74% on PanOptix Pro, and down again on PureSee. Each swing ran about 12 to 24 months and ended with a launch. What would strengthen or reverse it: Vivity Pro (FDA approved November 2026; United States launch January 2027) is the reversal mechanism; bundling and rebates slow the loop but have not stopped it. Jefferies' surgeon survey (21-Sep-26) expects Alcon to hold 66% and J&J to lose 130bp in 2027; 41% of surveyed ophthalmologists expect Alcon to lose the most share to new entrants. Financial effect in our base: implantables +1% in 2027 (H1 about -1%, H2 about +3% against a -2% comparison), +3% a year from 2028; share stabilising at about 60% to 65%, not returning to 70%+. Each 5 points of United States PC-IOL share is about $40 to $60 million of high-margin revenue.

Loop 2. Unity installed base (reinforcing, positive, slow) and the post-wave trough (balancing, negative, 2027). How it works: a console placement locks a clinic into proprietary cassettes and packs for seven to ten years; teaching-hospital placements (40% of United States teaching hospitals within a year) shape future surgeons' preferences; accounts with Alcon hardware historically grew Alcon lens use 10% against 2% in non-Centurion accounts (Redburn, 10-Oct-24). Evidence: consumables +6% cc to $789 million in Q3 2026; about 40% of consumables are tied to Alcon hardware. The Centurion precedent: 10% of the base upgraded in year one, 20% by year two, 65% only after five years, so the consumables uplift arrives over half a decade, not in a spike. The balancing side: equipment was pulled into 2025 to 2026 (+23% and +25% cc in Q1 and Q2 2026), comparisons are brutal in H1 2027, Japan's capital replacement is slower than planned, and clinics with 36-month paybacks keep their Centurions. Financial effect: consumables +5% a year (steady, the highest-confidence line), equipment +1% in 2027 (about -5% in H1, +3% to +4% in H2) and +3% a year after. The loop's value is persistence, not acceleration.

Loop 3. Contact lens capacity, share, scale and unit cost (reinforcing, positive). How it works: capacity in daily silicone hydrogels has historically been the gate to share (Alcon ceded leadership to J&J in 2012 when Dailies Total1, growing at 60% a year, was capacity-constrained; Cooper lost private-label contracts when MyDay was constrained); new DSM Flex lines produce several modalities on one line, lower unit cost and speed launches; lower cost and new products win share; share fills the lines. Evidence: record share of about 26%; Precision7 sales more than doubled; Singapore ramping to optimal speeds; the balancing lag is 24 to 36 months of under-absorption. Brake: Alcon missed its 2025 contact lens share targets because of international private label, and Asian contract makers (Visco Vision from 49 to 63 million lenses a month) are adding capacity fast. Financial effect: contact lenses +5% in 2027 and +5.5% from 2028; Vision Care segment margin rising from about 23.5% (2026) to about 27% (2030) as the lines fill.

Loop 4. Falling surgeon fees and premium conversion (reinforcing for the industry, balanced by affordability). Medicare fees -11% in 2026 and -4% proposed for 2027 make standard cataract surgery barely viable, so surgeons push premium lenses (over $2,000 of profit per premium lens against $450 for a monofocal, Morgan Stanley); United States premium penetration rose about 200bp in H1 2026. The balancing side: about 10% of patients defer or trade down to enhanced monofocals, 41% of surveyed surgeons see a modest pullback, and practices serving fixed-income patients cannot convert. For Alcon this loop currently pays rivals more than Alcon; it becomes an Alcon tailwind only once loop 1 is broken. Financial effect: built into implantables at +3% from 2028 (premium market growth in the high single digits times a stable share), not in 2027.

Loop 5. Bundling and pricing resentment (reinforcing, negative, slow). Restrictive rebate tiers and pack add-ons generate surgeon frustration; PE-owned ASCs (about 20% of United States cataract volume) buy vendor-agnostic; competitors (Bausch + Lomb, J&J) offer simpler, cheaper contracts; switching accelerates where switching costs are lowest (lenses, not consoles). Regulators add a brake: the FTC blocked LENSAR, and Alcon received a Department of Justice Civil Investigative Demand in July 2024 regarding discounts on surgical equipment servicing contracts (Q3 2026 interim report). Financial effect: it caps consumables price at +0.5% to +1% and is the mechanism by which loop 1 could outlast Vivity Pro; a regulatory restriction on bundling is a tail risk to consumables and IOL share, not in our base.

Loop 6. Buyback at a low multiple (reinforcing, positive) and its limit. A low share price makes each dollar of buyback retire more shares, lifting EPS growth by about 1 point a year; strong free cash flow ($693 million in H1 2026) funds it without constraining R&D or bolt-ons (Neurotech was paid while buying back). Unlike Carl Zeiss Meditec, where de-rating and falling ROIC forced a retreat from M&A, Alcon's cash flow keeps the loop positive. Financial effect: share count -0.8% to -1.3% a year (P-02).

Loop 7. Reinvestment of leverage (balancing, the most important for the margin). When leverage or windfalls appear, management reinvests them: two-thirds of the 2026 refund went back into R&D and launches; R&D is held at the top of 7% to 9%; acquired pipelines (Aurion about -80bp in 2025, Neurotech about -20bp in 2027) absorb the rest. This loop is why drop-through collapses in bad years and only reaches 38% to 45% in good ones. It will weaken only if the share price or a holder forces a margin-first stance. Financial effect: caps net margin expansion at 60 to 100bp a year in our base.

How the loops net out on the main lines:

  • Revenue: 2027 about +5.0% (H1 about +4.2%, H2 about +5.7%), 2028 +5.3%, 2029 +5.2%, 2030 +5.0%. A resolved IOL loop (share back to 70%) would add about 0.5 to 0.8 points a year from H2 2027; an unresolved one (implantables -3% through 2027) would take 0.7 points off 2027.
  • Margin: the balancing reinvestment loop dominates every other loop; the positive loops show up as margin from 2028 (contact lens absorption, consumables mix).
  • EPS: the buyback loop is the one loop that works regardless of the others.

Memo topic 4, Conservatism of guidance and record of delivery: the Rater's score

Group judgement

Guidance

D5149th percentile

Layer 1 · Guidance

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Alcon's guidance is specific, built from clear building blocks, and since 2026 set realistically. It is in line with the named post-results brokers for FY2026 and is pre-signalling a cautious 2027 (net margin under 100bp; growth at the low end of 6% to 8%). The upgrade record is not that of a sandbagger. The 2026 "raises" came from the share count, the refund and currency, while the operating guide was met at its original midpoint. The 2025 record (two cuts) shows guidance gives way when the market softens. Informal commentary is deliberately lowering the bar for March 2027 while keeping a product-led recovery narrative. Mid-term targets are stale and will be missed. Model implication: use management's FY2026 frame as the anchor (about $11.0 billion of sales, a core margin of about 21.1%, core EPS of about $3.50). For 2027, take the informal frame literally (+60 to +90bp, organic growth of about 5%, Neurotech adding 0.5 to 1 point). Assume no upgrade of mid-term targets, and a probable formal reset at some point, which would remove the overhang but lower the 2027 to 2029 path in management's own words.

C-68

To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

C-5057th percentile

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: for FY2026, guidance is in line with to slightly above the FactSet median (which carries stale low estimates) and in line with the named post-results brokers. For FY2027 there is no formal guide yet. Management's informal framework (net margin expansion under 100bp; growth at the low end of 6% to 8%) sits close to consensus on margin and slightly above consensus on revenue.

  • FY2026 revenue: the guide of about 6% cc plus about 0.9 points of currency is about $11.0 billion, against a FactSet median of $10.93 billion (about 1% lower, implying an implausible +1.7% Q4) and named brokers at $11.0 to $11.1 billion (in line).
  • FY2026 core margin: the guide of +100 to +150bp cc plus about 10bp of currency is about 21.0% to 21.4%, against consensus EBIT of $2,310 million on $10,931 million (21.1%), in line (Bernstein 21.1%).
  • FY2026 core EPS: the guide of +12% to +14% cc plus about 1.6 points of currency is about $3.49 to $3.55, against a FactSet median of $3.45 and mean of $3.44 (below the guide's low end) and named brokers at $3.47 to $3.52 (the low half; Bernstein $3.51; Equita $3.35). The reasons: some brokers haircut Q4 for the $40 million reinvestment and slower implantables, and the median includes stale estimates.
  • FY2027: consensus revenue of +5.6% ($11.55 billion), core EBIT margin of 21.8% (+70bp), core EPS of $3.83 (+11% on the $3.45 median, about +9.5% on a likely $3.50 actual). Management's informal frame of underlying leverage of 150 to 200bp less Neurotech and the refund (net under 100bp), with growth at "the low end of 6% to 8%", brackets this. The formal guide comes with FY2026 results (2 March 2027 per FactSet).
C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

C-5556th percentile

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: current guidance is realistic rather than conservative on revenue and mildly conservative on 2027 margin. Adjusted for weaker input forces than in 2021 to 2024 (slower procedures, premium share loss, tariffs, acquired dilution), it asks for a little less than the past delivered, which is appropriate.

  • Revenue: the past is +10%, +6% and +4% cc (2023 to 2025), with a long-term target of 6% to 8%. For 2026 the original guide was +5% to +7% cc on an assumed 3% to 4% market, and delivery is about 6% (the midpoint; not conservative). For 2027 to 2028, "the low end of 6% to 8%" includes Neurotech; with procedures at about 3% and implantables weak, organic growth of 5% to 5.5% is the realistic figure. Guidance is therefore at or slightly above what the input forces support.
  • Margin: the past is about +70bp a year underlying from 2019 to 2024, and -80bp in 2025. For 2026, +100 to +150bp (about +105bp before the refund). For 2027, under +100bp net. Adjusted for forces: the incremental savings in 2027 of about $80 million (about 70bp of sales) plus operating leverage on about 5% growth should give more than 100bp gross; Neurotech dilution, the non-recurrence of the refund (net about 15bp), launch spending (Vivity Pro, Tryptyr direct-to-consumer marketing) and chip and resin inflation take it back. "Under 100bp" therefore looks mildly conservative: a +60 to +90bp outcome is our base case, with upside if inflation eases.
  • Three years: there is no updated three-year guide; the 2023 Capital Markets Day targets are stale and too high.

To what extent is informal/qualitative company commentary above or below formal guidance and sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

F4418th percentile

Management is deliberately lowering the bar for the March 2027 guide while keeping the recovery narrative alive.

  • Below or cautious: "tracking at the low end" of 6% to 8%; market growth of about 3% to persist through 2027; competitor sampling "intense over the next two quarters"; lapping the 2026 Unity placements will "create a tough comparison"; Unity unit volumes "stable" with a mix shift to the lower-priced Unity CS; net margin expansion under 100bp; Neurotech "slightly dilutive" to 2027 core earnings.

C-73

What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

C4761st percentile

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: five features are material. (1) The core metric dominates: guidance, pay and broker models are on core figures that exclude about $1 billion or more a year of amortisation, restructuring and impairments, so IFRS EPS will remain far below core (about 35% below in 2025). The Consumer must model both and reconcile them. (2) Guidance in constant currency with currency given separately, plus no hedge accounting and no translation hedging, so reported results swing with the dollar and the franc. A 1% dollar move is $52 million of sales and $29 million of operating income. (3) Dual listing (SIX in CHF, NYSE in USD; reporting in USD): a stronger franc lowers the CHF value of USD earnings, so Swiss-line returns lag USD earnings growth when the franc rises. (4) The key competitive metric (United States PC-IOL share) is not disclosed: the market relies on Bernstein, Redburn, Jefferies surveys and Market Scope, so estimates of share vary by 5 to 8 points between sources and over time. (5) Stale mid-term targets ($12 billion and mid-20s by 2027) have not been formally reset; a new Capital Markets Day (none dated in the files) would be the natural moment and could itself be a catalyst, positive for credibility or negative for the numbers. Investor relations is stable (Dan Cravens), and the full-year results with next-year guidance come in late February or early March.

6c

Case simplicity

7 answers
Judgement68C-7947D-0280D-0366D-0465D-0544D-0680
Group judgement

Risks

A6893rd percentile

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts for the next 18 months are predictable. The path by which they are delivered, and the market's response to them, are much less so. The seven tests point the same way. The earlier answers agree with one another in mechanism and direction, and the forecast needs no single contested answer to be right (D-01). Our base asks Alcon to repeat its record, not to exceed it; about 70% of 2027 revenue growth, the whole margin gain and the buyback rest on strengths shown every year, and the premium lens assumption asks only for stabilisation (D-02). The base is diffuse; the variance is concentrated in two drivers that partly offset each other, because management flexes spending to deliver EPS (D-03). The cone for FY2027 core EPS is about 5% either side of $3.80, with the weight slightly on the lower half (D-04). The company's blocks rebuild its EPS to within about 1%, and every external force except surgeon economics can be sized (D-06).

The character of this predictability matters as much as its degree. It is the predictability of an annuity run to an EPS number: tight, with a slight negative skew and little upside. The forecast is rarely wrong by much and rarely surprises positively. That is exactly why three EPS beats in 2026 did not move the shares, and it is why the memo should not look to estimate changes for the return.

Where the predictability ends. (i) In the order of the evidence: the weak reports (March, May and August 2027) come before the easy comparisons (November 2027 to May 2028), and disclosure cannot show the premium lens line turning before August 2027 (D-05). (ii) In the 2028 margin, where our +100bp equals the best normal year on record. (iii) In a quarter of 2027 revenue growth that depends on two launches needing capabilities Alcon has not shown, and which cannot be tracked. (iv) Outside the company: currency and tariffs, each able to move EPS by more than the whole company-specific cone. (v) In the multiple, which needs both a company trigger and a sector re-rating, and Alcon controls one (D-07).

The steer for the Consumer. Treat the earnings as the solid leg of the case: $3.80 for 2027 within about 5%, about 1% below the updated brokers, on inputs we have not changed. Describe the riskiness as low in the fundamentals and high in the share price's response to one line. Define the monitoring test as United States implantables growth and third-party share, not reported implantables, which China will distort in the first half of 2027. And separate, in the return, the part the earnings deliver (about 16% over the 16 months to March 2028 with no change in the multiple) from the part that needs the two keys.

C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

D4750th percentile

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: the perimeter is moderately broad (two segments, five product lines, 56 countries of operation and more than 140 countries served) and widening into pharma and cell therapy. The diversification makes aggregate revenue quite predictable (within about plus or minus 1 point a year) but makes the margin less predictable, because new launches and acquired pipelines bring lumpy costs.

  • Composition (approximate 2026 shares of sales): consumables about 29%, contact lenses about 26%, Ocular Health about 18%, implantables about 16% to 17%, equipment about 10%. Regions: United States about 45% (consensus about 44% in 2026), international about 55% (Japan 6%, China 5% to 6%, Switzerland 1%).
  • Change: adding prescription dry eye and glaucoma drugs (Aerie), corneal cell therapy (Aurion), retina cell therapy for a rare disease (Neurotech), photobiomodulation (LumiThera) and diagnostics (Cylite). Exits are few (China Ocular Health commercial rights to Ocumension; PowerVision).
  • Predictability: high for consumables and contact lenses (annuity, low share volatility); medium for Ocular Health (launch-driven); low for implantables (share swings) and equipment (replacement waves). Margins are subject to launch spend (Tryptyr direct-to-consumer marketing when Medicare coverage widens; Vivity Pro), acquired R&D, tariffs and currency. For the model: revenue forecast error of about plus or minus 1 point a year; core margin error of about plus or minus 50bp a year.
D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

B+8084th percentile

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the expected change is small, and most of it is plausible on demonstrated strengths, because our base asks Alcon to repeat its own record and not to exceed it. About 70% of 2027 revenue growth, the whole of the 2027 margin gain and the share-count reduction rest on strengths shown repeatedly. The premium lens assumption asks only for what Alcon did in 2025 (stop a share loss with an upgraded lens), not for what it did in 2019 to 2021 (reverse one). The reliance on strengths that would have to be acquired is real but sits somewhere the narrative does not look: about a quarter of 2027 revenue growth comes from two launches (Tryptyr under Medicare, and ENCELTO) that need commercial capabilities Alcon has not yet shown. Consensus for 2028, where it is above us, asks for something Alcon has never done.

What the expected change is. From FY2025 to FY2028 our base has revenue growing 5.7% a year, the core margin rising from 19.8% to 22.6%, and core EPS rising 11.6% a year. The 2022 to 2025 record is 6.0%, about 70bp a year (2019 to 2024) and 11.1%. The "change" is continuity plus a recovery from the one bad year (2025).

Where the 2027 revenue growth of 5.0% comes from, and what each part relies on:

Source Points of group growth Strength relied on Demonstrated?
Consumables 1.5 Installed base; about 40% of consumables tied to Alcon hardware Yes, every year; +6% cc in Q3 2026
Contact lenses 1.3 Manufacturing capacity, record share of about 26%, United States price Yes; the price element only partly (below)
Ocular Health excluding Tryptyr and ENCELTO 0.8 Systane brand and formats Yes
Tryptyr 0.7 Prescription drug launch under Medicare Part D No
ENCELTO 0.4 Commercialising a retinal cell therapy No
Implantables and equipment 0.3 Vivity Pro; Unity CS Partly

The point the narrative misses is in the last three rows. The forecast relies very little on a premium lens recovery (0.2 points) and a good deal on pharmaceutical execution (1.1 points). Because the two launches carry launch costs and ENCELTO is dilutive, a shortfall there would cost revenue far more than EPS. That is the shape of Alcon's familiar result: revenue a little short, EPS delivered.

Demonstrated strengths, and the evidence.

  • The margin pace. Core margin rose 340bp from 2019 to 2024. Drop-through on extra revenue was 38% to 45% in each normal year. Our 2027 (+60bp at about 5% growth) is inside that record. The limit of the record matters, though: Alcon has never delivered more than 100bp of margin expansion in a year with cc growth of 5% or less and without one-off gains. The nearest such year was 2024 (+90bp, at cc growth of 5% to 6%). Our 2028 (+100bp at 5.3%) therefore equals the best normal year on record, and the FactSet consensus for 2028 (+130bp at 5.6%) exceeds it. Consensus has made this error before: in November 2024 it expected +145bp for 2025 (Morgan Stanley called it unrealistic) and the outcome was -80bp.
  • Delivering savings, and spending them. The 2019 to 2023 transformation programme cost about $425 million and delivered $300 to $325 million of run-rate savings on time; management reinvested them. The current $130 million programme repeats the pattern, and the company says the increment is designated to offset inflation. The demonstrated strength is delivery of the savings, and equally the habit of not letting them reach the margin.
  • Countering a rival lens within the same lens class. After Johnson & Johnson's Odyssey took United States premium share from above 80% to 66% (Q1 2025), PanOptix Pro (Q2 2025) restored it to about 73% within three quarters, and now makes up about 90% of PanOptix implants. The 2019 to 2021 swing (35% to above 80%) rested on a category-first product, which Vivity Pro is not. What is demonstrated, then, is the ability to convert Alcon's own users to an upgrade quickly and stop a loss for 12 to 24 months. Our base (share stabilising at 60% to 65%; implantables flat to +1% in 2027 and +3% after) asks for exactly that. Bernstein's +4% for 2027 asks for more. Precedent on timing points to the second half: premium lens launches take six to nine months to show in revenue, and Johnson & Johnson needed several quarters to stabilise its own share after earlier launches (Redburn).
  • The lock-in from rebates, with a limit the earlier sets do not describe. Clinics that keep about 90% of lens volume with Alcon earn rebates of about 30%, and a 10% fall in Alcon premium lens use cuts the quarterly rebate sharply (channel checks, September 2026). That slows switching. But free PureSee samples have already pushed some high-volume clinics below their threshold (channel check, 1-Jul-26). Once below it, the remaining Alcon lenses cost more and the reason to stay weakens. The rebate tier is a moat until it is breached and an accelerator afterwards. This is why share fell in steps, and why stabilisation is more plausible than recovery.
  • The mechanical items. The buyback (about $1.0 billion remaining), 98% fixed-rate debt and cash conversion near 90% to 100% of core net income are all demonstrated.

Strengths the thesis would need Alcon to acquire, and how much rests on each.

  • Prescription pharmaceuticals at scale under Medicare. Tryptyr runs at about 20% of Miebo's volume at the same point after launch (Bausch + Lomb; Deutsche Bank, 31-Oct-25). Bausch + Lomb reached about 80% coverage in both commercial and Medicare channels within two years; Tryptyr has more than two-thirds of commercial lives and no Medicare coverage before mid-2027. Only about 36% of new prescriptions were dispensed, the first month is free, and some patients stop within days because of the burning sensation on instillation. Our base needs about $80 million more revenue from Tryptyr in 2027.
  • Commercialising a cell therapy. Alcon has never done so. ENCELTO has helpful starting conditions (a permanent billing code, policies covering about 200 million lives, more than 700 patients enrolled, more than 100 surgeries by early 2026), but the nearest precedent, Luxturna, made about $250 million in five years against peak hopes of up to $750 million (Deutsche Bank, 10-Nov-26).
  • A leading non-diffractive optic, and an adjustable lens. Neither is in our base. They are in the bull case.
  • Letting operating leverage reach the margin. Not demonstrated in a year of 5% growth, and management says it prefers growth to margin. Our base does not need it; the FactSet consensus for 2028 and Pershing Square's 25% do.
  • Earning a return on acquired capital. Not demonstrated (PowerVision written off; Hydrus under royalty). Our base assumes acquisitions continue to dilute.

Precedents from peers. Self-help that depended on volume stalled when volume disappointed: Smith & Nephew raised its margin 240bp from 2022 to 2025 and then cut its 2026 guidance twice. Carl Zeiss Meditec shows the reverse case of leverage on a fixed base (margin from 23% to about 8%). Coloplast reset its 2030 targets in November 2026 after two years in which its share price halved. The cases that succeeded (Demant, Sonova) had a product cycle earning price at launch, which is the strength Alcon lacks in premium lenses today. Alcon's base case resembles none of the failures, because it does not depend on a volume recovery or on a cost programme reaching the margin.

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

B+6588th percentile

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the cone is narrow for earnings, with the skew modestly to the downside and concentrated in the first half of 2027. We put FY2027 core EPS between about $3.62 and $3.95 (5% below to 4% above $3.80) with roughly 80% confidence, FY2027 revenue growth between 4% and 6%, and the core margin between 21.2% and 21.9%. Earnings are predictable to within about 5%, which is better than most of the sector managed in 2026. The quarterly path is not predictable in the same way: year-on-year comparisons will distort four of the next six quarters, and the reports of March, May and August 2027 will each look weaker than the trend.

The cone (core EPS):

Bear Base Bull Our 80% range Against the updated brokers' median
FY2026 3.45 3.47 3.53 3.45 to 3.52 Base 0.7% below $3.49-$3.50
FY2027 3.60 3.80 4.00 3.62 to 3.95 Base 1.0% below $3.84
FY2028 3.95 4.27 4.55 4.00 to 4.50 Base 1.3% below $4.32-$4.33

Why about 5%, and why skewed down: the record. Over their lives the consensus estimates for FY2025, FY2026 and FY2027 have ended 12% to 16% below their peaks, almost all of it from the 2025 cuts. In the final year the errors are much smaller but one-sided. FY2025 missed its original sales and margin guides and finished at the low end of an EPS range that had already been cut. FY2026 consensus has moved only -1.0% in twelve months and the year will land on it, but with 3 to 4 points of help from the net tariff refund, the licensee milestone, currency and the larger buyback; the operating result was 3% to 4% short. In neither year did the outcome exceed what was expected a year before. A range of about 5% with the weight on the lower half is what the record supports, and it matches the sum of the drivers in D-03 once currency is included.

Why the 2027 cone should be no wider than that, and probably a little narrower than 2025's. (i) The 2027 frame was given four months early and is built from stated blocks; management is deliberately lowering the bar. (ii) The 2026 one-offs are already deducted in the bridge, so there is less to lose. (iii) The cost line will be flexed. (iv) The buyback is contracted. (v) The updated brokers are only 1% above us, so the distance between expectation and our base is small before the year begins.

The skew by period. The second set's quarterly figures are unchanged; this table adds two things it did not show.

Quarter (reported) Revenue Margin and EPS The reason
Q4 2026 (2-Mar-27) Neutral Slightly down About $40 million of reinvestment; a second negative implantables quarter (Bernstein: -1.5%); equipment against +18%
Q1 2027 (May 2027) Down Neutral Equipment against +23%; continued sampling; China procurement in force from January; Tryptyr still without Medicare
Q2 2027 (August 2027) Down Down, optically Equipment against +25%; the quarter also laps the $15 million licensee milestone, about 55bp of that quarter's margin, which the second set's quarterly shape omits: expect a core margin nearer 21.0% than its 21.5%
Q3 2027 (November 2027) Up Down, optically Implantables against -2% and equipment against +11%; Medicare coverage for Tryptyr; but the margin laps the refund (about 210bp)
Q4 2027 (about March 2028) Up Up, optically Easy comparisons; laps the Q4 2026 reinvestment
Q1 2028 (May 2028) Neutral Neutral The first quarter free of comparison effects

On the Q2 2027 point: the full year is unaffected beyond rounding, the return of record does not depend on the quarterly shape, and the second set itself marked the quarterly margins as low confidence, so we have not changed the quarterly inputs. The Consumer should simply expect Q2 2027 to print a lower margin than the model shows.

What the skew means for the case. Within the 18 months, the two reports that carry the most downside (March and May 2027) come first, and the three that carry the easy comparisons (November 2027 to May 2028) come last. The cone for the full year is close to symmetric; the order in which the evidence arrives is not. A holder is asked to sit through the weak prints to reach the strong comparisons.

What sits outside the cone. Currency (a 10% move in the dollar is about $290 million of operating profit, 12% of the total, with no translation hedging); a Section 301 tariff on Swiss and European imports ($50 to $150 million before mitigation); a large acquisition (2% to 4% dilution; management tried twice in 2025); a quality or supply event. Each is individually unlikely in a year. On the upside, outside the base: a larger buyback from a balance sheet heading to about 0.7x EBITDA by end-2028, and any easing of surgeon capacity. For 2028 specifically the skew in the margin is down (our +100bp equals the best normal year on record) and the skew from capital deployment is up.

Predictability, in one sentence. Revenue to within about 1 point, the margin to within about 40bp, EPS to within about 5%, with a better than even chance that the outcome is a little below base and not above it, and with nothing in the base that needs an unproven recovery.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

F4422nd percentile

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: disclosure is good for tracking the outputs of the thesis and weak for tracking its leading indicators, and over the next six months the calendar works against the investor. The company reports twice, and neither report can settle the question on which the share price turns. The 2 March 2027 report settles the margin question (the formal guide and its bridge) but covers a quarter that ended before Vivity Pro launched. The May 2027 report is the first to include the launch, but it covers a quarter that management has said will still see intense competitor sampling, and it will be clouded by China and by the equipment comparison. Monitoring the premium lens half of the case therefore depends on third-party evidence that arrives earlier and is noisier.

What the company gives every quarter: sales and cc growth for five product lines; contribution for two segments; core margin, core EPS and free cash flow; and guidance built from blocks (assumed market growth, currency effects on sales, margin and EPS, tariffs gross and net, savings, tax rate, share count, non-operating expense). In commentary, and not consistently: United States implantables growth, the price contribution in contact lenses, Tryptyr's share (about 6%) and prescribers (about 11,000), and PanOptix Pro's share of PanOptix implants (about 90%).

What it does not give: United States premium lens share (the market's test variable); Unity placements, the conversion of the installed base, or the price mix of consoles; Tryptyr's sales and net price; ENCELTO's sales and loss; China's sales; the nature of the Q4 2026 reinvestment; savings by cost line; profitability by product line.

The calendar to mid-May 2027, and what each event can and cannot tell us:

When Event What it can tell us
December 2026 Cooper's results United States contact lens channel inventory and the industry's price intentions for 2027
January 2027 Vivity Pro launch; United States list-price changes; China procurement in force; healthcare conferences Whether Alcon repeats a contact lens price increase (visible in retailer price checks); management's tone
Mid-January 2027 Johnson & Johnson Q4 results United States surgical vision growth (+14% in Q3 2026): whether its momentum persists
February 2027 Bausch + Lomb results Premium lens growth; Miebo and Xiidra; the timing of its own new lens
2 March 2027 Alcon Q4 2026 and the FY2027 guide The margin bridge; whether the Q4 reinvestment recurs; Neurotech's dilution; the RxSight milestones (Jefferies expects details with the guide); the pace of the buyback. Nothing on Vivity Pro beyond anecdote
Mid-April 2027 Johnson & Johnson Q1 results The first hard figure after the launch, about three weeks before Alcon reports
April to May 2027 Surgeons' annual meeting; broker surveys; market-share data for Q1 Surgeon preference between Vivity Pro and PureSee. Alcon's own contralateral study against PureSee (165 patients) reports only in late 2027
Early to mid-May 2027 Alcon Q1 2027 The first implantables figure with Vivity Pro; equipment against +23%; Tryptyr before Medicare coverage. Not a clean reading

What follows from this for the Consumer:

  1. The market's test variable will be contaminated in the first half of 2027. Reported implantables growth will carry about 2 points of drag from China's price cuts, which says nothing about Vivity Pro. The first set's invalidation test ("implantables below zero for two quarters") will be met with the Q4 print in any case, and in the first half of 2027 it could stay met because of China alone. The memo should define the test as United States implantables growth together with third-party share data, not reported implantables growth.
  2. The best leading indicator is a competitor's disclosure. Johnson & Johnson's United States surgical vision growth is published about three weeks before each Alcon report and has moved inversely to Alcon's implantables. A fall from +14% toward mid-single digits would be the earliest sign that sampling is fading.
  3. The margin half of the thesis is highly trackable, and soon. One dated event (2 March 2027) resolves the largest open modelling question, which the second set valued at $0.05 to $0.08 of 2027 EPS. Management's candour helps here: giving the 2027 frame in November has already removed most of the scope for surprise.
  4. Tryptyr and ENCELTO cannot be tracked reliably. Prescription audits miss the digital pharmacy through which much of Tryptyr's volume passes (Bernstein), and the company gives no sales figure for either product. A quarter of 2027 revenue growth is therefore the least observable part of the forecast.
  5. The first report that can show a clean recovery is outside the six months. Management's "two more quarters of intense sampling", said on 11 November 2026, covers Q4 2026 and Q1 2027. The first quarter beyond it is Q2 2027, reported in August 2027.

The disclosure is good enough to tell us quickly if the case is breaking (the outputs are reported in detail every quarter). It is not good enough to tell us early that the case is turning.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

A8098th percentile

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the internal transmission is clear, among the clearest in the sector. What the company says it will achieve can be traced through named drivers to five revenue lines, a bridge of margin items stated in basis points, and three guided items below the operating line, and the result reproduces guided EPS to within about 1%. The chain is loose in three places: the reinvestment decision that sits between operating leverage and the reported margin; the profitability of individual product lines; and the route from a Unity placement to revenue and to cash. The external transmission is clear for currency, tariffs and China procurement, where the arithmetic is given, and unclear for the most important external force, surgeon economics, which pushes in two directions at once.

The chain, with what each link is worth to FY2027 core EPS:

What the company says Operational driver Line Worth
Market growth about 3% Procedures; installed base of about 30,000 consoles Consumables 1 point of growth = $32 million = about 0.8% of EPS
Implantables return to growth in early 2027 United States premium lens share; Vivity Pro Implantables 5 points of share = $40 to $60 million = about 1.8%
Units stable, mix to Unity CS Placements; console price Equipment 5 points of growth = $57 million = about 0.9%
Price and premium mix United States list price; daily lenses Contact lenses 1 point of price = $30 million = about 1.3%
Medicare coverage; launch Prescriptions; net price; implants Ocular Health $40 million of Tryptyr = about 0.5%
Underlying leverage of 150 to 200bp, net under 100bp Refund -50 to -55bp; milestone -10 to -15bp; Neurotech -20bp; inflation -20bp; currency -10bp Core margin 10bp = $12 million = 0.5%
Savings of $130 million run rate Headcount; shared services Operating expenses $80 million in 2027, of which under half reaches the margin
Tax about 20%; about 487 million shares in 2026; non-operating $210 to $230 million Below the line 1 point of tax = 1.25%; 1 million shares = 0.2%; $10 million = 0.4%

The test of clarity is whether the blocks rebuild the result. They do: the second set reproduced the low end of the FY2026 EPS guide from the stated margin, tax, interest and share count to within two cents. Any quarterly variance can be attributed to a named block.

Where the internal chain is loose:

  1. Between operating leverage and the margin sits a decision. Management states the underlying leverage and the deductions, but how much it reinvests is its own choice and is disclosed afterwards (two-thirds of the refund, announced with the Q3 results). This is why drop-through on extra revenue was 2% in 2025 and 39% in 2026 on a similar cost structure. The link from revenue growth to margin is discretionary in any one period. The looseness makes the margin harder to forecast and EPS easier (D-03).
  2. Profitability by product line is not disclosed. Only two segments report contribution. Implantables are described as the highest-margin line (premium lenses carry gross margins of about 90% in the United States, per Morgan Stanley) and equipment as the lowest, without figures. The margin effect of the 2027 mix (less equipment, flat implantables, more pharmaceuticals) can only be estimated.
  3. From a Unity placement to revenue and cash. Consoles are sold outright, by instalment or by lease, or placed with no upfront payment and recovered through higher pack prices over contracts of about three years (annual report; channel checks, September 2026). Revenue is divided between equipment and consumables by stand-alone prices. Two consequences follow, and both are our inference, not disclosure. Reported equipment and consumables growth partly reflect the form of the contract. And revenue can run ahead of cash: receivables grew faster than sales in 2025 (+$206 million) and working capital stays at about 33% of revenue. This is the main reason for our request for cash-flow detail.
  4. Smaller gaps. Tryptyr's net price after discounts, and ENCELTO's economics, are undisclosed. The core adjustments are traceable but large (about $0.6 billion a year from 2027).

The external transmission:

  • Currency: explicit. A 1% move in the dollar is $52 million of sales and $29 million of operating profit, about 1.25% of EPS, because costs in francs and dollars exceed revenues in those currencies. Guidance is given in cc with the currency effect stated separately.
  • Tariffs: explicit. Gross exposure of $80 to $100 million a year; $40 million net in 2026 after the $58 million refund; duties of 10% to 12.5% assumed. The one unknown is whether the proposed Section 301 levies are enacted.
  • China procurement: the arithmetic is clear and the date is now known. Price cuts of 30% to 50% from January 2027 on $125 to $143 million of implantables: $40 to $70 million of revenue before any volume response, or about $0.07 to $0.11 of EPS if volume does not respond.
  • Input inflation: partly clear. Brokers put chips and resins at about 20bp; the company gives no figure and does not hedge commodities.
  • Surgeon economics: unclear, because the force acts in opposite directions on different lines. The final Medicare rules for 2027 cut surgeons' professional fees again (about 3% to 4% across the relevant codes, per Jefferies, 5-Nov-26) while raising the facility payment to surgery centres and hospital outpatient departments by 2.3%. The surgeon who chooses the lens is paid less, which pushes premium conversion, where Alcon's share of the increment is currently falling. The facility that buys consoles and packs is paid slightly more, which is a little better for capital budgets than the earlier sets assume, although surgeons and administrators still describe upgrades as deferrable. Surgeon capacity caps procedure volumes throughout. The net effect on Alcon's EPS cannot be traced to a line with confidence; about 41% of sales sit under fixed procedure reimbursement.
  • Competitors: observable but not quantifiable in advance. Johnson & Johnson's sampling and pricing (PureSee about 15% below Vivity in Europe) reach Alcon through implantables growth with a lag of one or two quarters.

The judgement in practice. An analyst can rebuild Alcon's EPS from public blocks, can say within a week of a result why it differed, and can size every known external force except surgeon economics. That supports both the predictability of the forecast and the ability to monitor it.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

C+3372nd percentile

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: sentiment risk is high. The shares have a demonstrated habit of falling on implantables headlines regardless of the EPS beat, and the stock sits in a sector the market is actively selling.

  • The company's own reaction function: -12% over two days after Q1 2026 (implantables +1% on easy comparisons, despite an EPS beat and a raise); lower after the Q2 beat-and-raise; -4% after Q3. The Q4 print (early March 2027) will likely show a second negative implantables quarter.
  • Sector and peer read-across: a peer warning (Cooper cut its guide; Coloplast's reset; Carl Zeiss Meditec's restructuring); Johnson & Johnson's or Bausch + Lomb's quarterly IOL growth (Johnson & Johnson's United States surgical +14% in Q3); third-party share data (Market Scope, broker surveys: 41% of surgeons expect Alcon to lose the most share in 2027); RxSight news.
  • Market and theme: rotation out of healthcare if technology money returns (the environment digest flags that healthcare became a home for technology money "that can leave again"); a discount-rate move; risk-off in which only the "cleanest" defensives are bought.
  • Investor base: United States buy-side investors are "meaningfully less convinced" than European ones; a holder such as Pershing Square exiting would weigh on the shares; the dual listing transmits United States sentiment to Zurich.
  • Magnitude: 10% to 15% moves on single events are precedented. A sector capitulation could take the 24-month-forward multiple to about 14x to 15x (its five-year low is 14.95x).

C-61How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?D5035th percentilefull answer under 4b Management quality
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

D3834th percentile

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: the record is mixed. Alcon upgrades readily when non-operating items allow (share count, tariff refund, currency), cuts when the market softens (twice in 2025), and narrows or trims late in the year. It has not yet shown a pattern of upgrades driven by organic outperformance.

  • 2025: guidance cut twice (sales to about 4%; the margin missed its 20% to 21% range), which damaged credibility (shares -8% on the first cut).
  • 2026: set conservatively in February; raised in May (EPS on the buyback's share count); raised in August (margin and EPS, about half from the $20 million of refund reaching operating income); trimmed in November (margin midpoint -15bp, EPS midpoint -50bp; sales narrowed to the midpoint).
  • Nature: the operating guide (sales and margin before the refund) was essentially met at its original midpoint, not beaten. The narrative of "sandbagging, then upgrading" that brokers adopted in mid-2026 does not survive the full-year evidence: the original 2026 guide was accurate on sales and slightly generous on underlying margin.
  • Chief executive behaviour: candid about competition; prefers to keep long-term targets ("committed to 6% to 8%"; "mid-20s") rather than reset them, so upgrades of mid-term targets are unlikely and quiet slippage is more likely.
C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

D4749th percentile

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: Alcon has a persistent pattern of small revenue misses and EPS beats, the "hollow beat" pattern. It met guidance in benign years, missed and cut in the one comparable soft environment (2025), and is meeting 2026 with non-operating help.

  • Last year (four quarters): Q4 2025: revenue slightly below consensus ($2,700 million against $2,710 million), EPS miss ($0.78 against $0.79). Q1 2026: revenue slightly below ($2,690 million against $2,700 million), EPS beat (+5%). Q2 2026: revenue beat (+0.3%; +7% cc against +6%), EPS beat (+11%). Q3 2026: revenue below (+5% cc against +6%; less than 1%), EPS beat (+3%, refund-aided).
  • Three years: revenue below consensus in each of Q2 2024 (-1.9%), Q3 2024 (-1.6%), Q2 2025 (-2.1%) and Q3 2025 (-0.7%); EPS beats in each (+1%, +9%, +7%, +3%). Across the nine quarters on file, revenue missed in seven and EPS beat in seven. FY2024 met guidance (cc sales at the high end; a slight EPS beat); FY2025 missed (guidance cut twice; sales of $10.32 billion against an original $10.40 to $10.50 billion; core margin of 19.6% to 19.8% against 20% to 21%; EPS of $3.07 against $3.05 to $3.15, a midpoint miss).
  • Five years: strong in the 2021 to 2023 recovery (+10% cc in 2023; core EPS +22% in 2023).
  • Comparable conditions: 2025 (soft procedures, competitive launches, currency and tariffs) is the analogue for 2027, and Alcon missed then. Today's conditions are similar but guidance is set lower.
  • For the model: expect revenue to land 0% to 1% below consensus and EPS within about 1% of consensus, with costs flexed to deliver it.
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

D4945th percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the forecasts deserve high weight for 2026 and 2027 (an EPS error band of about plus or minus 4% to 5%), medium weight for 2028 (plus or minus 7%) and low weight for 2029 to 2030 (plus or minus 10% or more). Revenue should be leaned slightly down against consensus in every year, because Alcon has missed consensus revenue in seven of the last nine quarters; near-term EPS should be held close to consensus, because Alcon flexes costs to deliver EPS; and the 2028 and later margin should be leaned down, because Alcon has never met a mid-term margin target on time. Our base already embodies that lean (revenue in line to 0.5% below consensus; 2027 EPS 1% below; 2028 EPS 3% below); we would not lean it further without H1 2027 implantables evidence.

The record, by horizon:

  • Quarterly (nine quarters on file): revenue below consensus in seven (by 0.3% to 2.1%), EPS above consensus in seven (by 1% to 11%). The "hollow beat": costs, the share count, the refund and currency are used to deliver EPS when revenue falls short. Q3 2026 is typical: revenue +5% cc against +6% expected, EPS beat by 3% with refund help.
  • Annual guidance: FY2024 met (cc sales at the high end, a slight EPS beat). FY2025 missed and cut twice: sales of $10.32 billion against an original $10.40 to $10.50 billion, core margin 19.8% against 20% to 21%, EPS $3.07 against $3.05 to $3.15 (below the midpoint). FY2026 set conservatively in February, raised in May (share count) and August (refund), trimmed in November; the operating guide (sales about 6%, margin before the refund) was met at its original midpoint, not beaten.
  • Mid-term targets: the 2023 Capital Markets Day ambitions ($12 billion of sales and mid-20s core margin by 2027) will be missed by about $0.4 billion and about 300bp; on our base the mid-20s margin arrives about 2030, three years late. The 6% to 8% growth target is "maintained" but the company is "tracking at the low end".
  • The comparable environment: 2025 (soft procedures, competitor launches, currency, tariffs) is the closest analogue for 2027, and Alcon missed in it. The difference now is that guidance is set lower and management is visibly lowering the bar for March 2027 (Redburn "welcomed" the conservative tone).

What that means for each line of our forecast:

Which forecasts carry the most and least predictive weight, by driver: highest weight on consumables (+5%; installed-base annuity, plus or minus 1 point), contact lenses (+5% to +5.5%; plus or minus 1 point, the risk being the price repeat), the share count, tax and interest; lowest weight on implantables (plus or minus 5 points of United States PC-IOL share over 12 to 18 months, which is about plus or minus 3 points of implantables growth), equipment (plus or minus 5 points of growth on a lumpy replacement wave) and Ocular Health launches (Tryptyr's ramp with Medicare Part D; ENCELTO's undisclosed economics).

Why we do not lean further down: the 2027 framework is the first one management has set after the 2025 credibility damage, and it is built from stated blocks that the Consumer can audit (underlying leverage, refund, Neurotech, savings). The near-term risk is that the formal guide on 2 March 2027 is set at or below our base, not that our base is too high. The risk sits in 2028, where no guidance exists and consensus assumes the best version of every driver at once.

Growth rate

Group judgement

Financial forecasts

C-5354th percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial model that follows from the foundations is that of a dependable 10% to 11% core EPS compounder whose upside is capped by its own reinvestment policy and whose downside is cushioned by its annuity. Seventy per cent of revenue (consumables, contact lenses, Ocular Health) grows about 5% to 8% a year with little error; the remaining 30% (implantables and equipment) is where the cone is wide, and in 2027 both are near zero growth. Operating leverage is real (40% to 50% drop-through), but management has spent about half of it in every year on record, and the 2027 savings programme is earmarked for inflation, so net margin gains of about 60 to 100bp a year are the realistic pace, reaching the mid-20s around 2030. Price protects the margin rather than lifting it. Below the line, the move from a flat to a falling share count and from rising to falling interest turns a historical headwind into a tailwind of about 1.5 to 2 points of EPS growth a year, and the balance sheet de-levers toward net cash, which creates a capital-deployment question (buyback or acquisition) that is a larger swing factor for 2028 to 2030 EPS than interest cost. For the Consumer's third and fourth sets: the 2026 and 2027 forecasts are predictable (EPS within about 5%) and close to consensus, so the near-term case does not rest on beating estimates; 2028 carries the risk, because consensus assumes the best outcome on implantables, equipment, savings and dilution at once. The forecast's direction of error is slightly down on revenue and on post-2027 margin, and the single variable that would move every line at once is premium IOL share after Vivity Pro, readable from May 2027.

B6477th percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: below the operating line, the business moves from a headwind to a modest tailwind. From 2021 to 2025, rising interest ($120 million to $204 million), a flat share count (about 490 to 495 million) and, in 2026, a tax step-up (17.4% to 19% to 20% under Pillar 2) took about 1 to 3 points a year off EPS growth relative to core EBIT growth. From 2027 the tax rate is stable at about 20%, interest drifts down and the buyback retires a net 0.8% to 1.3% of shares a year, so EPS grows about 1.5 to 2 points faster than core EBIT. Free cash flow converts about 95% to 98% of core net income, and the core-to-IFRS gap, still about $1.0 to $1.2 billion of pre-tax items in 2026, narrows to about $0.6 billion from 2027 as the PowerVision impairment and most restructuring fall away.

C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

C6661st percentile

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: there is plausible scope to add about 250 to 350bp to the core margin by 2029 to 2030 (reaching about 23.5% to 24.5%) and to sustain 10% to 12% EPS growth. It would come from a few big areas (gross-margin mix and productivity; SG&A leverage; the fading of acquisition dilution) rather than many small ones. It is congruent with the strategy's "operational excellence" second horizon and with peer precedent, but it takes about two years longer than the 2023 Capital Markets Day promised.

  • Gross-margin mix and productivity (+100 to +150bp by 2029): Ocular Health (Systane preservative-free, Tryptyr), premium contact lenses (daily silicone hydrogels, Precision7, Total30 multifocal toric), the higher-priced Unity consumables cycle, and lower contact lens unit costs as the Singapore and Grosswallstadt lines fill.
  • SG&A leverage (+100 to +150bp): revenue growth of 5% to 6% on a semi-fixed commercial base, plus shared services and the savings programme.
  • Fading of acquisition dilution (+30 to +60bp from 2028): if Aurion (approval targeted for late 2028) and Neurotech reach commercial scale; offset by any new bolt-ons.
  • Below operating profit: a net share count reduction of about 1% a year, flat interest, a core tax rate of about 20%. Small but mechanical.
  • Peer precedents: Cooper sustains about 250bp more operating margin on a comparable mix; Sonova lifted normalised EBITA margin from 20.9% to 23.7% in two years; Alcon itself added 340bp from 2019 to 2024.
  • Congruence: management explicitly prioritises "sustainable incremental revenue growth" over margin and keeps R&D at the top of its range, so margin delivery will be steady rather than aggressive unless the share price or a large holder (Pershing Square) forces a faster pace.

D4230th percentile

Judgement: the engine has decelerated from its H1 2026 peak to about its normal rate, and it is heading slightly below normal for 2027. The input force (market procedure growth and Alcon's premium IOL share) is itself slowing. The deceleration is moderate in aggregate but sharp in the highest-margin line.

  • Normal rate: about 6% a year (6.0% compound growth on the brokers' basis from 2022 to 2025; constant-currency growth of +10%, +6% and +4% in 2023, 2024 and 2025; the long-term target is 6% to 8%).
  • Last twelve months: constant-currency growth of about +7% (Q4 2025), +6% (Q1 2026), +7% (Q2) and +5% (Q3); the FY2026 guide is about 6%. So the last twelve months are at the normal rate, but the latest quarter is below it, and consensus 2027 (+5.6% reported, including about 0.5 to 1 point from Neurotech) implies organic growth of about 4.5% to 5%.
  • Input forces slowing: global cataract procedures at about 3% against a historical 4% to 5% (United States flat); implantables went from +1% to -2% cc, with share falling from 73% to 74% to about 66%; the equipment wave halved (+25% to +11%) and faces tough 2027 comparisons; Ocular Health went from +12% to +8%. Steady: consumables (about +5%) and contact lenses (+5%).
  • What would reverse it: implantables back above +3% for two to three quarters (Vivity Pro); surgeon capacity easing; Medicare Part D lifting Tryptyr.

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

C-5252nd percentile

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the next four years look like a slightly slower, slightly more profitable and more shareholder-friendly version of the last four. Revenue grows about 5% a year (against 6.0% a year from 2022 to 2025 and 6.6% a year from 2019 to 2025 including the Covid rebound); core margin gains about 70bp a year from 2027 (against about 70bp a year from 2019 to 2024 and a reversal in 2025); and core EPS compounds at about 10.5% a year from 2026 to 2030 (against 11.1% a year from 2022 to 2025), with the buyback replacing the revenue growth that is lost. The composition shifts: less from equipment and implantables, more from consumables, contact lenses and Ocular Health; less from revenue, more from margin and the share count. The trough is 2027 (revenue +5.0%, EPS +9.5%), and the 2028 step (EPS +12.4%) is the year most exposed to error.

Where the net impact comes from (core EPS growth decomposed, 2027 and 2028):

Driver 2027 2028
Revenue at constant margin +5.0 points +5.3 points

What has changed against the past, and what has not:

  • Revenue: the replacement equipment wave and the 2023 post-Covid recovery that lifted 2023 to 2026 are gone; implantables, once the growth engine of the PanOptix era, contribute only 0.2 to 0.5 points a year. Consumables (29%) and contact lenses (26%) deliver about 2.8 points a year mechanically; Ocular Health (18%) adds about 1.5 points (Tryptyr, ENCELTO, Systane). The organic rate settles at about 4.6% to 5.0%, with acquisitions adding 0.3 to 0.5 points. This is below the 6% to 8% target, at the bottom of which management says it is tracking.
  • Margin: the drivers are the same as 2019 to 2024 (gross margin mix and productivity, SG&A leverage), but the drags are more numerous (tariffs that did not exist before 2025, acquired pipelines, the Hydrus royalty) and the savings programme is defensive. The path to 23.9% by 2030 is about 70bp a year, matching the 2019 to 2024 pace; the 2023 promise of mid-20s by 2027 is about three years late.
  • EPS: about one-tenth of growth now comes from the share count (nothing did before 2026), and the tax step-up of 2026 is a one-time cost already in the base.
  • Cash: conversion is structurally higher than before 2024, and the balance sheet de-levers toward net cash by 2030 unless management deploys it (P-02).

The asymmetry the Consumer should carry into the third set: revenue has a narrow cone (plus or minus 1 point a year) because 70% of it is annuity; the margin has a wider one (plus or minus 50 to 100bp) because it depends on management's reinvestment choices and on three external items (tariffs, chips and resins, currency: a 1% dollar move is $52 million of sales and $29 million of operating income). Most of the forecast risk is therefore in the margin line, and most of the margin risk is in 2028.

Forecasts and upgrades

Probably upgrades this financial year to

D3642nd percentile

Memo topic 7, Scope for upgrades over 6 to 12 months: the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

C-4457th percentile

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Our assessment is almost fully reflected in guidance and in estimates, and only partly reflected in the narrative. Guidance already says what we think: growth at the low end, net margin expansion under 100bp, EPS growth of about 10% (I-01). The updated brokers are about 1% above us in each year (I-02). There is therefore no gap in the numbers for the case to exploit, and the memo should not present Alcon as a stock whose forecasts will force upgrades over the next 6 to 12 months. The gap is in what the market believes the numbers are worth. The market prices a premium lens share story at a floor multiple. Our assessment is of a predictable annuity compounding EPS at about 10% a year, with one contested line worth 17% of sales and a balance sheet heading toward net cash. That gap is favourable. It can close only through the narrative, and the narrative cannot change before August 2027 on the company's part, and at no known date on the sector's (I-03, D-07).

The steer on timing. Over the next six months the expected news is neutral to negative on all three counts: the guide resets the old targets in substance; revisions stay optically negative; and the implantables figure prints negative again, with China added. Nothing in our assessment says the shares must fall on news that is this well signalled. Nothing says they must rise. From month 6 to month 18 the balance improves: the first clean premium lens reading in August 2027, easy comparisons from the Q3 2027 report, revisions that have stopped falling in substance, and a 2028 guide that should sit on our base.

The steer on the stage of the stock's life. Bottoming, not yet turning up. The dashboard's pinned ratings (14-Nov-26) say the same: valuation A (the 1st percentile of ten years), sell-side earnings momentum F, technical momentum D (the price is 7% above its 200-day average, but that average has not risen in any of the last twelve months, and relative strength is at the 44th percentile). Turning up would need estimates to stop falling and one result that the price rewards.

The steer on the return. The model's +52.9% to 31 March 2028 should be explained in two parts of very different reliability. The earnings part (about +16% including dividends, on our EPS) is the predictable leg of the case and compounds with time. The multiple part (+30.8% in the model) needs both keys described in D-07. On our EPS, in dollars, and with our rough weights for March 2028:

Multiple on two-year-forward EPS at 31 March 2028 Total return from $73.21 Our rough weight
14.5x with estimates 5% lower (the first set's trough) about -8% 15% to 20%
Unchanged at about 17.4x on our EPS about +16% 35%
20x (Alcon's historical premium restored to an unchanged sector) about +33% 35%
The guideline 22.51x (the sector re-rates as well) about +50% 10% to 15%

The weighted figure is about +20% to +25% over 16 months, a little under half the return of record. Two things in that table matter more than the weights. First, time helps the holder: because two-year-forward EPS grows about 15% over the period, the trough multiple applied in March 2028 gives only about -8%, where the same multiple applied today gives the first set's -15% to -25%. Second, over three years the earnings leg is larger (about +30% on our EPS with no change in the multiple) and the re-rating has longer to arrive, so the case is stronger over three years than over 18 months.

What the memo should say, topic by topic. Guidance is realistic, not conservative, and the record of raising it is non-operating (topic 4). The scope for our forecasts to force upgrades over 6 to 12 months is small (topic 7). Momentum in guidance, revisions and the narrative is currently negative (topic 8). The fundamental risk is low and the share-price risk is concentrated in one line (topic 9). The stock is bottoming (topic 10). And the guideline multiple is reachable only with the sector's help: the company-earned level is about 19.5x to 20x (topic 11).
