Earnings per share is core diluted EPS (the brokers' basis), computed as (core EBIT less net financial items) times (1 less the core tax rate) over weighted diluted shares of 487, 482, 478, 474 and 467 million; core net income $1,691, $1,830, $2,042, $2,232 and $2,420 million. Against FactSet median consensus: +0.6%, -0.8%, -3.0% and -1.9% (FY2029, six brokers). Free cash flow is operating cash flow less capex including intangibles and less lease payments, before acquisitions and dividends: about 95% of core net income in 2026 and 2027 (restructuring cash of about $120 million and $80 million; working capital absorbing about $180 to $230 million a year) and about 97% to 98% thereafter. If the model's share count differs from ours because it prices the buyback differently, EPS will differ by the same proportion.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Alcon (ALC-CH; SIX and NYSE): the global eye care leader, reporting in USD in two segments: Surgical (implantables / intraocular lenses including presbyopia-correcting lenses, surgical consumables and custom packs, phaco and vitreoretinal equipment such as the Unity platform) and Vision Care (contact lenses; Ocular Health, including Systane OTC drops, the prescription dry eye drug Tryptyr and, since October 2026, Neurotech's ENCELTO retinal cell therapy).

Request: T-D1

Peers: Johnson & Johnson Vision (the PureSee, Odyssey and Acuvue rival whose premium lens launches took share from Alcon in 2016, 2024 and 2026); Bausch + Lomb (enVista Envy premium lenses recovering after the 2025 recall; Miebo and Xiidra dry eye); The Cooper Companies (CooperVision, the contact lens margin and capacity benchmark); Carl Zeiss Meditec (ophthalmic equipment and lenses, running the ProfitUp cost programme after a de-rating); RxSight (light-adjustable lens entrant and now Alcon licensor); STAAR Surgical (premium out-of-pocket implantable lens maker Alcon tried to buy); Sonova and Demant (patient-paid premium device makers with short platform cycles and recent margin expansion); Smith & Nephew (a medtech self-help and margin recovery precedent under activist pressure) Operating conditions: an intense competitive share attack in premium intraocular lenses; a post-replacement-wave trough in capital equipment after a strong installed-base upgrade cycle; United States Medicare surgeon fee cuts (11% in 2026, 4% proposed for 2027 per the proposed physician fee schedule); China volume-based procurement for intraocular lenses; United States tariffs on Swiss and EU imports and memory chip and resin inflation; a weak or strong US dollar against a Swiss franc cost base; surgeon capacity shortages capping procedure volumes Inputs: Can Alcon return implantables to positive growth within two to three quarters of the January 2027 United States launch of Clareon Vivity Pro (FDA approved November 2026), as its PanOptix launch did in 2019 to 2021 and PanOptix Pro did in 2025, and how long did past premium lens share swings (Alcon's United States presbyopia-correcting share 55% in 2009, 35% in 2019, over 80% in 2021, 66% in Q1 2025, about 66% in late 2026 per Rothschild & Co Redburn, 11-Nov-26) take to reverse? Has Alcon ever delivered more than 100 basis points of core operating margin expansion in a year with constant-currency growth of 5% or less and without one-off gains, and what did its drop-through on incremental revenue look like in 2021 to 2026? Can the $130 million restructuring programme (costs raised to $230 million, November 2026) reach the margin, or will it be absorbed by inflation and tariffs as the company indicates? Is Alcon's contact lens pricing (about 4 points of 5% growth in Q2 2026 per the chief executive; two United States increases in H1 2026) repeatable in 2027? Does the Unity installed base demonstrably lift consumables and lens pull-through at account level (Centurion precedent: 10% of the base upgraded in year one, 65% over five years)? Can Tryptyr's ramp with 2027 Medicare Part D coverage match Bausch + Lomb's Miebo, given tolerability reports? Which of these strengths Alcon has demonstrated, and which it would need to acquire (non-diffractive EDOF optics, adjustable lenses, cell-therapy commercialisation)? Focus: Whether Alcon's expected 2027 to 2028 recovery in implantables growth and in core margin rests on strengths it has already shown in past product cycles and cost programmes, or on strengths it has not yet demonstrated, especially in non-diffractive premium optics and in letting operating leverage reach the margin rather than reinvesting it.

Request: T-D2

Peers: Johnson & Johnson Vision (premium lens share-taker and contact lens leader); Bausch + Lomb (premium lens and dry eye competitor with low surgical margins); The Cooper Companies (contact lens peer with destocking-driven volatility); Carl Zeiss Meditec (equipment-cycle and China procurement exposure, a de-rating precedent); Sonova and Demant (premium device cycles and share mean reversion); Coloplast and Smith & Nephew (European medtech franchises whose estimates and multiples fell when growth slowed); Glaukos and Sight Sciences (MIGS competition and the Hydrus royalty) Operating conditions: competitor sampling and free trials in United States premium lenses; lapping a strong equipment upgrade year (Alcon equipment +23% and +25% in constant currency in Q1 and Q2 2026); a mix shift to a lower-priced standalone console (Unity CS); new prescription drug launches dependent on Medicare Part D coverage; tariff escalation risk (proposed Section 301 levies of 12.5% on Swiss and 10% on EU imports); currency moves against a Swiss franc and US dollar cost base (a 1% dollar move equals $52 million of sales and $29 million of operating income per Alcon); bolt-on acquisitions that dilute core margins in their first years Inputs: How concentrated are the drivers of the difference between Alcon's 2027 to 2028 financials and its 2022 to 2025 financials: implantables growth and United States premium lens share; 2027 equipment revenue after the Unity wave; contact lens price; the Tryptyr and ENCELTO ramps; the non-recurrence of the $58 million Q3 2026 tariff refund and whether the roughly $40 million reinvested in Q4 2026 recurs; Neurotech dilution (about 20 basis points in 2027 per Bernstein, 12-Nov-26); memory chip and resin inflation; the share count under the $1.5 billion buyback? Historically, how wide has the range of outcomes been for Alcon's annual revenue growth and core margin against the guidance and consensus set a year earlier (2019 to 2026), and how often have medtech forecasts that depended on one product launch or one margin step been met? What events widened or narrowed the dispersion of outcomes for peers in comparable situations (Carl Zeiss Meditec in China, Cooper's destocking, Smith & Nephew's 2026 guidance cut)? What is the status and potential effect of the US Department of Justice Civil Investigative Demand of July 2024 on Alcon's surgical equipment servicing discounts, and of any other regulatory challenge to its bundling? Focus: Whether the gap between Alcon's 2028 consensus (core EBIT margin 23.1% and EPS of $4.40 per FactSet, 14-Nov-26) and its 2025 base rests on one or two drivers (premium lens share and margin) or on many, and how skewed the plausible range of 2027 outcomes is around the first half of 2027.

Request: T-I1

Peers: Johnson & Johnson Vision (quarterly United States surgical vision growth as the read-across on premium lens share); Bausch + Lomb (premium lens and dry eye guidance under a new chief executive); The Cooper Companies (contact lens market growth, pricing and destocking); Carl Zeiss Meditec (equipment and China procurement guidance); RxSight (light-adjustable lens utilisation and guidance); Glaukos and Sight Sciences (MIGS); Sonova and Demant (patient-paid premium device guidance in the same sector) Operating conditions: a company that cut guidance twice in 2025 and then raised in 2026 on share count, a tariff refund and currency; a preliminary 2027 framework given early (underlying leverage of 150 to 200 basis points, net core margin expansion under 100 basis points, growth at the low end of 6% to 8%); a FactSet FY2026 revenue median of $10,931 million (14-Nov-26) against named post-results brokers at $11.0 to $11.1 billion; sector-wide negative revisions and de-rating; competitor product launches; reimbursement changes in the United States, Germany and China Inputs: How likely is Alcon's formal FY2027 guidance (due with FY2026 results, 2 March 2027 per FactSet) to be set above, at or below its November 2026 framework and current consensus (FY2027 revenue $11,548 million, core EBIT $2,512 million, core EPS $3.83, FactSet median 14-Nov-26), and what would each require? Will FY2026 core EPS land within the guided +12% to +14% constant currency (plus about 1.6 points of currency), and what Q4 2026 core margin and core EPS does the guide imply while about $40 million of tariff refund is reinvested? How likely are FY2027 and FY2028 consensus EPS (net revisions -2.6% and -2.4% over three months per FactSet) to be cut, held or raised at the Q4 2026, Q1 2027 and Q2 2027 results, and how likely is the 2023 Capital Markets Day ambition ($12 billion of sales and mid-20s core margin by 2027) to be formally reset? Focus: Whether the March 2027 guide and the first two 2027 quarterly results (May and August 2027) are more likely to trigger further cuts to FY2027 and FY2028 consensus or to stabilise it, and which single disclosure (implantables growth after Vivity Pro, the 2027 margin bridge, or equipment) would decide it.

Request: T-I2

Peers: Johnson & Johnson Vision; Bausch + Lomb; The Cooper Companies; Carl Zeiss Meditec; RxSight; STAAR Surgical; Sonova; Demant; Coloplast; Smith & Nephew (European and United States eye care and medtech names whose narratives investors compare with Alcon's) Operating conditions: a sector trading at a median 17.1 times earnings at the 2nd percentile of its own range (22-Oct-26); rotation of technology money into and out of healthcare; high government bond yields; a market that has sold Alcon after each 2026 EPS beat (shares down about 12% over two days after Q1 2026 and 4% after Q3 2026); patient-paid premium product cycles; reimbursement pressure Inputs: How widely discussed, by which sell-side analysts and which investors or commentators, since when and with what view, are these dynamics at Alcon: the loss of United States presbyopia-correcting lens share to J&J's PureSee and whether Vivity Pro reverses it; the gap between underlying operating leverage (150 to 200 basis points) and net margin expansion (under 100 basis points) in 2027, and the reinvestment of the 2026 tariff refund; the 2027 equipment trough after the Unity wave and the Unity CS mix shift; the repeatability of United States contact lens price increases and softer international contact lens pricing; the Tryptyr ramp and tolerability; Neurotech and ENCELTO dilution and economics; the rising Pillar 2 tax rate; the buyback and the use of balance-sheet capacity (net debt 1.41x EBITDA at Q3 2026 per FactSet) between buybacks and acquisitions; the size of core-to-IFRS adjustments (restructuring, impairments, acquired R&D outside core); Pershing Square's holding and its argument for margins of 25% or more; the de-facto miss of the 2023 Capital Markets Day targets? Question: How are Alcon's Tryptyr (acoltremon) prescriptions, net pricing, gross-to-net discounts, Medicare Part D formulary placement for 2027, refill persistence and tolerability evolving against Bausch + Lomb's Miebo and Xiidra, and what are the launch timing, pricing, reimbursement, patient-identification approach and expected revenue and operating loss of Neurotech's ENCELTO under Alcon ownership?

Request: OWN-5

Question: What are the timing, scope, price cuts and winners of China's 2026-2027 national volume-based procurement round for intraocular lenses and related ophthalmic consumables, how did Alcon, Carl Zeiss Meditec, Johnson & Johnson and domestic makers such as EyeBright fare in earlier rounds by price and volume, and what share of Alcon's China sales (about 5% to 6% of group sales) is exposed?

Request: OWN-6

Question: Over rolling six-month windows since 2023, how closely have Alcon's share price and its 24-month-forward P/E moved with European medtech (the B.5 medtech consumables peer group, the STOXX Europe health care sector), with United States ophthalmic peers (The Cooper Companies, Bausch + Lomb, RxSight, Glaukos) and with the Swiss market and the Swiss franc, and around which events (Alcon's quarterly results, guidance cuts in 2025, competitor launches, sector rotations) did Alcon's moves become idiosyncratic?

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 16 November 2026, 07:51 to 08:21 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Alcon (ALC-CH): Third and fourth sets of questions - the predictability of the forecasts, and how far guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect our assessment

Written 2026-11-16 for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo. Money in USD and on the company's core (adjusted) basis unless stated; "cc" means constant currency; "bp" means basis points. "Our base" means the second set's forecast inputs, which this set leaves unchanged. "The model" means the Financial Analyst's model after update 1. "The FactSet median" is the consensus in the financials block (14-Nov-26). "The updated brokers" are the individual estimates FactSet shows in that block, most dated 11 or 12 November 2026, with the rows the block repeats under different labels counted once.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Orientation for the Consumer: the five judgements that matter most, which figures to use, and the model's flagged gaps

The five judgements that matter most for the memo:

  1. The earnings are predictable; the share price is not; and the two have different drivers. About 70% of 2027 revenue growth and all of the 2027 margin gain rest on strengths Alcon has shown in every normal year since the spin-off (the consumables and contact lens annuity, margin gains of 60 to 100bp a year, a cost line that management flexes to deliver EPS, the buyback). We put FY2027 core EPS within about 5% of $3.80 with roughly 80% confidence, skewed modestly down. The multiple, by contrast, is set by one product line worth about 17% of sales and by a sector that is itself de-rated.
  2. Our forecasts sit about 1% below the updated brokers in every year from 2026 to 2028, and the "2028 stretch" that the first two sets found in consensus is about half the size they reported. The FactSet medians ($3.45, $3.83, $4.40) mix fresh and stale estimates. The updated brokers' medians are about $3.49 to $3.50, $3.84 and $4.32 to $4.33. Against those, our base ($3.47, $3.80, $4.27) is 0.7%, 1.0% and 1.3% lower. Neither an upgrade cycle nor a deep downgrade cycle is in prospect. Estimates are not where this case makes or loses money.
  3. The information that arrives over the next six months is skewed toward confirming known negatives, not resolving them. The results on 2 March 2027 will probably show a second negative implantables quarter and a formal 2027 guide that confirms the old mid-term targets are missed. The first print after the Vivity Pro launch (May 2027) carries continued competitor sampling, the hardest equipment comparison of the cycle and, new in this set's research, China's second procurement round from January 2027. The first quarter that can show a clean recovery is Q2 2027, reported in August 2027.
  4. The narrative is complete on the one negative and thin on almost everything else. Every covering house discusses the premium lens share loss. Very few discuss what actually generates the EPS (the annuity, the managed cost line, the tax step-up that hid part of 2026's operating growth) or the balance sheet's path toward net cash. The market treats Alcon as a premium lens share story and prices it at the 1st percentile of its ten-year multiple range.
  5. The return of record leans on a re-rating that our own forecasts do not, by themselves, earn. Of the model's +52.9% to 31 March 2028, 30.8 points come from the multiple moving from 17.2x to the guideline 22.51x. Our base has United States premium lens share stabilising, not recovering, and no large-cap peer in the dashboard sector with rising estimates trades above 21.5x today. On our reading (D-07), a company-driven re-rating in an unchanged sector reaches about 19.5x to 20x; the remainder needs the sector to re-rate. On our EPS that is a total return of about +33% by March 2028, against about +16% with no re-rating and about -8% at a trough multiple with estimates 5% lower.

Which EPS figures to use:

Core EPS FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 Basis
Our base (second set, unchanged) 3.47 3.80 4.27 Company basis; weighted diluted shares
The model's forecasts of record 3.55 3.87 4.35 FactSet revenue base; undiluted shares
FactSet median 3.45 3.83 4.40 17, 19 and 17 brokers; partly stale
Updated brokers' median (our count) 3.49 to 3.50 3.84 4.32 to 4.33 12, 14 and 12 estimates
Company guidance 3.49 to 3.55 none yet none +12% to +14% cc plus about 1.6 points of currency

The model's figures run about 2% above our base for reasons of basis, not of view (below). When the memo states our position against consensus it should say "about 1% below the updated brokers", not the model summary's "+2.8%, +1.0%, -1.1%".

The model's flagged gaps, explained.

Free cash flow (flagged in all five years: the second set's cross-check of $1,610, $1,740, $1,980, $2,180 and $2,370 million against the model's $1,263, $1,478, $1,679, $1,875 and $2,072 million). The model's figure can be reproduced exactly from the inputs: core EBITDA, less net financial items, less tax at the core rate on core pre-tax profit, less capital spending, less lease payments, less the rise in working capital (for FY2027: 3,164 - 215 - 463 - 720 - 105 - 183 = 1,478). The second set's figure was not built that way. It is core net income multiplied by a conversion ratio (about 95% in 2026 and 2027, 97% to 98% after) taken from reported cash flow. The two differ by about $300 million in every year because reported operating cash flow contains items for which the model's build has no line. The test is to run the model's formula on a reported year. For FY2025 it gives about $1,210 million, against about $1,530 million actually generated on the same definition (operating cash flow of $2,271 million, less capital spending including intangibles of $641 million, less lease payments of about $100 million). That $320 million difference is the same size as the flagged gap; the first nine months of 2026 give the same answer (about $290 million before restructuring payments). The files do not itemise it. Its probable parts are: (i) share-based pay, a cost in core earnings but not a cash cost (probably $150 million or more a year; the figure is not in our files); (ii) tax, because the model charges 20% on core profit, whereas the tax on the items excluded from core (about $0.6 billion a year of amortisation and restructuring from 2027) is largely deferred, not paid (roughly $100 to $140 million a year); less (iii) restructuring payments, which are outside core but are cash (about $120 million in 2026 and $80 million in 2027).

Conclusion: the gap does not show that any forecast input is wrong, and we have changed none. It shows that the model's free cash flow is a stricter measure than the cash the company reports, and that the second set's cross-check was asserted from a ratio and not reconciled to its own inputs. The Consumer should use the cross-check, shaded slightly. Conversion on its definition was about 87% of core net income in FY2024, about 100% in FY2025 and about 88% in the first nine months of 2026 (before a seasonally strong fourth quarter), so 92% to 95% is a fairer central range than 95% to 98%: about $1.56 to $1.61 billion for 2026, $1.68 to $1.74 billion for 2027 and $1.88 to $1.94 billion for 2028. Net debt therefore falls faster than the model shows: to about 0.7x core EBITDA at end-2028, against the model's 0.95x. The second set's strategic conclusion (about $3.5 to $4.5 billion of unused debt capacity by 2028 at 2x EBITDA) holds on either figure. One caution on a source: the "free cash flow" line in the FactSet block ($1,474 million in FY2024 and $1,562 million in FY2025) is after dividends paid, although the block describes it otherwise (operating cash flow less fixed-asset capital spending is $1,604 million and $1,728 million). Conversion ratios in the first two sets that rest on that line understate conversion before dividends.

Earnings per share (not flagged, but the model is 2.3%, 1.8%, 1.9%, 2.3% and 2.7% above the cross-check from FY2026 to FY2030). There are two causes, and neither is a view. (i) The model applies our growth rates to FactSet's standardised FY2025 revenue ($10,401 million), which is 0.8% above the company's ($10,319 million). Because depreciation and interest are entered as fixed amounts, that becomes about 1.1% at net income. (ii) The model's EPS is on undiluted shares (about 483 million in FY2026), where the company guides, and brokers use, weighted diluted shares (about 487 million): 0.8%. From FY2029 a third cause adds 0.5 to 0.8 points: the model appears to price the larger assumed buybacks at about today's share price, although its own return table has the price more than 50% higher by then, so it retires more shares than the second set assumed. FY2026 also carries a slightly lower tax rate in the model (18.9% against 19.3%).

The phasing note ("the periods were moved by +15.2 points"). FactSet's reported EBITDA for Q2 2026 includes the PowerVision impairment (an 11.9% margin), so the nine reported months sum to about 21% where the core figure is about 26.7%. To reach our core figure for the year, the model must put about 41% into Q4 2026. The year is right; the quarter is an artefact. This is also why the template's trailing split of the return misleads, and the two-year-forward split should be used, as the summary says. For a related reason (the history is on FactSet's standardised lines, after amortisation of acquired intangibles, and the forecast is on the core basis) the model's history columns (EPS of $1.88 in FY2025) are not comparable with its forecast columns: the summary's "+88.6%" EPS growth for FY2026 is, against core EPS of $3.07, growth of +13% on our base.

For the Financial Analyst. The Financial Analyst should (i) show the comparison with consensus after removing the 0.8% difference between FactSet's revenue base and the brokers' basis, or rebase forecast revenue on the brokers'-basis actual of $10,319 million; (ii) show EPS on weighted diluted shares beside the undiluted figure, and price forecast buybacks along the model's own share-price path; (iii) add a line to the cash flow for the difference between core earnings and cash (share-based pay, deferred tax on the core adjustments, restructuring payments), calibrated on FY2025 and the first nine months of 2026; and (iv) replace FactSet's operating EBITDA with the company's core figures for the three reported quarters of 2026, so that Q4 2026 does not absorb the impairment.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of ALC-CH with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-13 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Alcon (ALC-CH; SIX and NYSE): the global eye care leader, reporting in USD in two segments: Surgical (implantables / intraocular lenses including presbyopia-correcting lenses, surgical consumables and custom packs, phaco and vitreoretinal equipment such as the Unity platform) and Vision Care (contact lenses; Ocular Health, including Systane OTC drops, the prescription dry eye drug Tryptyr and, since October 2026, Neurotech's ENCELTO retinal cell therapy)

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Alcon (ALC-CH; ALC on NYSE), what multiple of forward earnings have the shares, and comparable medtech stocks, commanded at each stage of a competitive or share-loss worry, and how far and how fast did the multiple recover once the worry cleared? Specifically: (a) Alcon's forward price-to-earnings multiple, and its premium or discount to European medtech and to the STOXX Europe 600 Health Care index, at each stage of its United States presbyopia-correcting lens cycles (the 2019 to 2021 recovery on PanOptix and Vivity; the late-2024 to 2025 loss of share to Johnson & Johnson's Odyssey and the recovery on PanOptix Pro; the 2026 loss of share to PureSee), with dates; (b) the same for Sonova and Demant around their 2024 to 2026 hearing aid platform launches, for Straumann, for The Cooper Companies after its September 2026 destocking, for Coloplast after its November 2026 reset of 2030 targets, and for Smith & Nephew and Carl Zeiss Meditec: how many quarters of evidence the market required before it re-rated each stock, what multiple each reached relative to the sector and to its own history, and what happened where the sector as a whole stayed de-rated; (c) the target multiple and valuation method each covering broker currently uses for Alcon (for example Jefferies' $85 target on 23 times 2027 earnings per share, 11-Nov-26; Bernstein's CHF 72.75; Deutsche Bank's CHF 73; Rothschild & Co Redburn; Morgan Stanley), how each justifies its multiple, and what each says must happen for Alcon's multiple to recover; and (d) what brokers and strategists say about the outlook for the multiple of the European medtech sector itself (Deutsche Bank put it at about 18 times forward earnings on 26-Aug-26)? Give dates and figures for every precedent, and separate the re-ratings that were driven by the company from those driven by the sector.

Request: OWN-2

Question: What are Alcon's (ALC-CH; ALC on NYSE) company-reported figures, quarter by quarter from Q1 2024 to Q3 2026 and for the full years 2023, 2024 and 2025, for: (a) net sales by product line (implantables, consumables, equipment/other, contact lenses, ocular health) in USD and as constant-currency growth, and United States against international growth for implantables; (b) core operating income and core operating margin, core gross margin, the core effective tax rate, core diluted earnings per share and the weighted diluted share count; (c) cash flow: net cash from operating activities, purchases of property, plant and equipment, purchases of intangible assets, lease payments (principal and interest), equity-based compensation expense, income taxes paid, interest paid, cash paid for restructuring and for acquisitions, the company's own measure of free cash flow, and the movements in trade receivables, inventories and trade payables; (d) what management and brokers have said about the bridge from core net income to free cash flow, about why trade receivables grew faster than sales in 2025, and about how Unity consoles placed without an upfront payment (cost-per-use or premium-priced Custom Pak contracts, instalment sales and leases) are recognised in revenue and collected in cash; and (e) how Alcon's free cash flow conversion and working capital compare with those of The Cooper Companies, Bausch + Lomb, Carl Zeiss Meditec, Sonova, Demant, Coloplast and Smith & Nephew? Quote each figure with its source document and date, and state which of these figures the company does not disclose.

Request: OWN-3

Question: What do investors, as distinct from sell-side analysts, say and do about Alcon (ALC-CH; ALC on NYSE) since May 2026: (a) Pershing Square (its interim report of 12-Aug-26 and its third-quarter 2026 letter to shareholders of 3-Nov-26): the size and timing of its holding, its stated thesis, what it expects on margins, capital returns and the pipeline, and whether it has engaged with Alcon's management or board; (b) other fund managers' letters, conference remarks, interviews and social-media commentary that mention Alcon, with the view each takes; (c) changes in the largest shareholders, in United States against European and Swiss ownership, in short interest and in index membership; (d) what brokers report about investor positioning and sentiment and about the questions investors ask, in particular after the Q1, Q2 and Q3 2026 results, when the shares fell despite earnings-per-share beats; and (e) which of the following subjects investors discuss and which they do not: the loss of United States premium intraocular lens share to Johnson & Johnson's PureSee and the January 2027 launch of Vivity Pro; the 2027 core margin bridge and the reinvestment of the 2026 tariff refund; the equipment cycle after the Unity launch; contact lens pricing; Tryptyr and ENCELTO; the $1.5 billion buyback and the use of the balance sheet; the difference between core and reported earnings; China's volume-based procurement of intraocular lenses; and the 2023 Capital Markets Day targets for 2027 ($12 billion of sales and a mid-20s core operating margin)? Report the same for investor sentiment toward the European medtech sector and toward Johnson & Johnson Vision, Bausch + Lomb, The Cooper Companies and Carl Zeiss Meditec, so that it is clear what investors say would have to change for them to buy Alcon.
